# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title conference Model

class Author {
    papers : List<Paper>
    calculateAcceptanceRate() : double
    countSubmittedPapers() : int
    submitPaper(paper : Paper) : void
}
class CoChair {
    makeFinalDecision(paper : Paper, decision : Grade) : int
}
class Paper {
    decision : Grade
    title : String
    type : PaperType
    reviews : List<ReviewAssignment>
    isAllReviewsConsistent() : boolean
}
class ReviewAssignment {
    feedback : String
    grade : Grade
    paper : Paper
}
class Reviewer {
    assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>
    calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore() : double
    calculateUnsubmittedReviews() : int
}
class User {
    name : String
    roles : List<UserRole>
}
abstract class UserRole {
    id : String
}
enum Grade {
    UNDECIDED
    ACCEPT
    REJECT
}
enum PaperType {
    RESEARCH
    EXPERIENCE
}

Author --|> UserRole
Author --> "*" Paper : papers
CoChair --|> UserRole
Paper --> "*" ReviewAssignment : reviews  'opposite: paper
ReviewAssignment --> Paper : paper  'opposite: reviews
Reviewer --|> UserRole
Reviewer --> "*" ReviewAssignment : assignments
User *-- "*" UserRole : roles
@enduml
```

52373 [main] INFO  du.ustb.sei.mde.mwe2.EcoreGenerator  - LLM Response:
{org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@697d6ded (name: calculateAcceptanceRate) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary:
  Calculates the acceptance rate of papers submitted by this author,
  defined as the ratio of accepted papers to total submitted papers.
  If the author has no papers, returns 0.00.
Algorithm:
  1. Let total = papers.size().
  2. If total == 0, return 0.00.
  3. Let accepted = 0.
  4. For each p in papers:
       if p.decision == Grade.ACCEPT, increment accepted.
  5. Return accepted / total as a double.
Related Features:
  - papers (List<Paper>)
  - Paper.decision (Grade)
Related Classes:
  Paper, Grade
Input:
  None.
Output:
  double - acceptance rate in the range [0.00, 1.00].
  Example: 10 papers, 6 accepted -> 0.60.
Pre-condition:
  - The Author instance is properly initialized.
  - papers list may be empty; each Paper.decision is a valid Grade value
    (UNDECIDED, ACCEPT, or REJECT).
Post-condition:
  - Returns 0.00 if the author has no papers.
  - Otherwise returns accepted_count / total_count, where only papers
    whose decision is ACCEPT are counted as accepted.
  - The state of the Author and its papers is unchanged., org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@160b4809 (name: makeFinalDecision) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary:
  Sets the final decision for a paper if and only if all of the following
  are satisfied: (a) the paper has at least 3 reviews, (b) no review has
  grade UNDECIDED, and (c) all submitted reviews are consistent
  (either all ACCEPT or all REJECT). Returns 1 on success, otherwise -1
  and leaves the paper's decision unchanged.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate paper != null and decision is either ACCEPT or REJECT.
  2. If paper.reviews.size() < 3, return -1.
  3. For each r in paper.reviews:
       if r.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED, return -1.
  4. If !paper.isAllReviewsConsistent(), return -1.
  5. paper.decision = decision.
  6. Return 1.
Related Features:
  - Paper.decision (Grade)
  - Paper.reviews (List<ReviewAssignment>)
  - Paper.isAllReviewsConsistent()
  - ReviewAssignment.grade (Grade)
Related Classes:
  Paper, ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
  paper : Paper   - the paper whose decision is to be made. Non-null.
  decision : Grade - the final decision; must be ACCEPT or REJECT
             (not UNDECIDED).
Output:
  int - 1 if the decision was successfully applied, -1 otherwise.
Pre-condition:
  - paper is a valid, non-null Paper.
  - decision is ACCEPT or REJECT (not UNDECIDED).
Post-condition:

  Determines whether all reviews for this paper are consistent, i.e.,
  they are exclusively all ACCEPT or exclusively all REJECT. Returns
  false if the paper has no reviews or if any review is UNDECIDED.
Algorithm:
  1. If reviews is null or reviews.isEmpty(), return false.
  2. Let hasAccept = false, hasReject = false.

       if r.grade == Grade.ACCEPT, hasAccept = true.
       if r.grade == Grade.REJECT, hasReject = true.
  4. If hasAccept && hasReject, return false.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title conference Model

class Author {
    papers : List<Paper>
    calculateAcceptanceRate() : double
    countSubmittedPapers() : int
    submitPaper(paper : Paper) : void
}

    makeFinalDecision(paper : Paper, decision : Grade) : int
}

    decision : Grade
    title : String
    type : PaperType
    reviews : List<ReviewAssignment>
    isAllReviewsConsistent() : boolean
}

    feedback : String
    grade : Grade
    paper : Paper
}
class Reviewer {
    assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>
    calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore() : double
    calculateUnsubmittedReviews() : int
}
class User {
    name : String
    roles : List<UserRole>
}
abstract class UserRole {
    id : String
}
enum Grade {
    UNDECIDED
    ACCEPT
    REJECT
}
enum PaperType {
    RESEARCH
    EXPERIENCE
}

Author --|> UserRole

CoChair --|> UserRole
Paper --> "*" ReviewAssignment : reviews  'opposite: paper
ReviewAssignment --> Paper : paper  'opposite: reviews
Reviewer --|> UserRole
Reviewer --> "*" ReviewAssignment : assignments
User *-- "*" UserRole : roles
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Author::calculateAcceptanceRate
Summary:
  Calculates the acceptance rate of papers submitted by this author,
  defined as the ratio of accepted papers to total submitted papers.
  If the author has no papers, returns 0.00.
Algorithm:
  1. Let total = papers.size().
  2. If total == 0, return 0.00.
  3. Let accepted = 0.
  4. For each p in papers:
       if p.decision == Grade.ACCEPT, increment accepted.
  5. Return accepted / total as a double.
Related Features:
  - papers (List<Paper>)
  - Paper.decision (Grade)
Related Classes:
  Paper, Grade
Input:
  None.
Output:
  double - acceptance rate in the range [0.00, 1.00].
  Example: 10 papers, 6 accepted -> 0.60.
Pre-condition:
  - The Author instance is properly initialized.
  - papers list may be empty; each Paper.decision is a valid Grade value
    (UNDECIDED, ACCEPT, or REJECT).
Post-condition:
  - Returns 0.00 if the author has no papers.
  - Otherwise returns accepted_count / total_count, where only papers
    whose decision is ACCEPT are counted as accepted.
  - The state of the Author and its papers is unchanged.

Author::submitPaper
Summary:
  Adds a paper to the author's list of submitted papers.
Algorithm:
  1. If paper != null, add paper to papers.
  2. Otherwise, do nothing (or throw an IllegalArgumentException).
Related Features:
  - papers (List<Paper>)
Related Classes:
  Paper
Input:
  paper : Paper - the paper to be submitted. Must be non-null and properly
          initialized (title, type, reviews list).
Output:
  void - no return value.
Pre-condition:
  - paper is a valid Paper instance (non-null).
Post-condition:
  - paper is appended to the author's papers list.
  - The same paper instance may appear only once in the list.

Author::countSubmittedPapers
Summary:
  Counts the total number of papers submitted by this author.
Algorithm:
  1. Return papers.size().
Related Features:
  - papers (List<Paper>)
Related Classes:
  Paper
Input:
  None.
Output:
  int - the total number of papers in the author's papers list (>= 0).
Pre-condition:
  - The Author instance is properly initialized.
Post-condition:
  - Returns the number of papers; the Author state is unchanged.

       if a.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED, increment count.

Reviewer::calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore
Summary:
  Converts the reviewer's submitted review grades (excluding UNDECIDED)
  into a numerical average score in the range [0.00, 1.00], where
  ACCEPT counts as 1 and REJECT counts as 0. Returns 0.00 if there are
  no submitted reviews.
Algorithm:
  1. Let total = 0, submittedCount = 0.
  2. For each a in assignments:
       if a.grade == Grade.ACCEPT, total += 1, submittedCount += 1.
       else if a.grade == Grade.REJECT, total += 0, submittedCount += 1.
       (UNDECIDED is ignored.)
  3. If submittedCount == 0, return 0.00.
  4. Return total / submittedCount as a double (within error 0.001).

  ReviewAssignment, Grade

  an error margin of 0.001. 0.00 if no submitted reviews exist.

  - The Reviewer instance is properly initialized.
  - assignments list may be empty; each ReviewAssignment.grade is a
    valid Grade value.
Post-condition:

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title conference Model

class Author {
    papers : List<Paper>
    calculateAcceptanceRate() : double
    countSubmittedPapers() : int
    submitPaper(paper : Paper) : void

    calculateUnsubmittedReviews() : int
}

    name : String
    roles : List<UserRole>
}

    id : String
}

    UNDECIDED
    ACCEPT
    REJECT

    RESEARCH
    EXPERIENCE

ReviewAssignment --> Paper : paper  'opposite: reviews

User *-- "*" UserRole : roles
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Reviewer::calculateUnsubmittedReviews
Summary:
  Calculates the number of unsubmitted reviews assigned to this
  reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is
  UNDECIDED. Returns 0 if all reviews are submitted or if the
  reviewer has no assignments.
Algorithm:
  1. Let count = 0.
  2. For each a in assignments:
       if a.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED, increment count.
  3. Return count.
Related Features:
  - assignments (List<ReviewAssignment>)
  - ReviewAssignment.grade (Grade)
Related Classes:
  ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
  None.
Output:
  int - number of assignments whose grade is UNDECIDED (>= 0).
Pre-condition:
  - The Reviewer instance is properly initialized.
  - Each ReviewAssignment.grade is a valid Grade value.
Post-condition:
  - Returns the count of UNDECIDED reviews; the Reviewer state is
    unchanged.

Reviewer::calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore
Summary:
  Converts the reviewer's submitted review grades (excluding UNDECIDED)
  into a numerical average score in the range [0.00, 1.00], where
  ACCEPT counts as 1 and REJECT counts as 0. Returns 0.00 if there are
  no submitted reviews.
Algorithm:
  1. Let total = 0, submittedCount = 0.
  2. For each a in assignments:
       if a.grade == Grade.ACCEPT, total += 1, submittedCount += 1.
       else if a.grade == Grade.REJECT, total += 0, submittedCount += 1.
       (UNDECIDED is ignored.)
  3. If submittedCount == 0, return 0.00.
  4. Return total / submittedCount as a double (within error 0.001).
Related Features:
  - assignments (List<ReviewAssignment>)
  - ReviewAssignment.grade (Grade)
Related Classes:
  ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
  None.
Output:
  double - average score in the range [0.00, 1.00], accurate within
  an error margin of 0.001. 0.00 if no submitted reviews exist.
  Example: 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT -> 0.75.
Pre-condition:
  - The Reviewer instance is properly initialized.
  - assignments list may be empty; each ReviewAssignment.grade is a
    valid Grade value.
Post-condition:
  - Returns the computed average; the Reviewer state is unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
Paper::isAllReviewsConsistent
Summary:
  Determines whether all reviews for this paper are consistent, i.e.,
  they are exclusively all ACCEPT or exclusively all REJECT. Returns
  false if the paper has no reviews or if any review is UNDECIDED.
Algorithm:
  1. If reviews is null or reviews.isEmpty(), return false.
  2. Let hasAccept = false, hasReject = false.
  3. For each r in reviews:
       if r.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED, return false.
       if r.grade == Grade.ACCEPT, hasAccept = true.
       if r.grade == Grade.REJECT, hasReject = true.
  4. If hasAccept && hasReject, return false.
  5. Otherwise (hasAccept XOR hasReject, but not both), return true.
Related Features:
  - reviews (List<ReviewAssignment>)
  - ReviewAssignment.grade (Grade)
Related Classes:
  ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
  None.
Output:
  boolean - true if reviews exist, none is UNDECIDED, and all are
           exclusively ACCEPT or exclusively REJECT; false otherwise.
Pre-condition:
  - The Paper instance is properly initialized.
  - Each ReviewAssignment.grade is a valid Grade value.
Post-condition:
  - The Paper state is unchanged; only a boolean answer is produced.

# Operation Specifications
CoChair::makeFinalDecision
Summary:
  Sets the final decision for a paper if and only if all of the following
  are satisfied: (a) the paper has at least 3 reviews, (b) no review has
  grade UNDECIDED, and (c) all submitted reviews are consistent
  (either all ACCEPT or all REJECT). Returns 1 on success, otherwise -1
  and leaves the paper's decision unchanged.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate paper != null and decision is either ACCEPT or REJECT.
  2. If paper.reviews.size() < 3, return -1.
  3. For each r in paper.reviews:
       if r.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED, return -1.
  4. If !paper.isAllReviewsConsistent(), return -1.
  5. paper.decision = decision.
  6. Return 1.
Related Features:
  - Paper.decision (Grade)
  - Paper.reviews (List<ReviewAssignment>)
  - Paper.isAllReviewsConsistent()
  - ReviewAssignment.grade (Grade)
Related Classes:
  Paper, ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
  paper : Paper   - the paper whose decision is to be made. Non-null.
  decision : Grade - the final decision; must be ACCEPT or REJECT
             (not UNDECIDED).
Output:
  int - 1 if the decision was successfully applied, -1 otherwise.
Pre-condition:
  - paper is a valid, non-null Paper.
  - decision is ACCEPT or REJECT (not UNDECIDED).
Post-condition:
  - If 1 is returned, paper.decision is set to the provided decision.
  - If -1 is returned, paper.decision remains unchanged.
  - CoChair's own state is unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

```plantuml
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
CoChair::makeFinalDecision
Summary:
  Sets the final decision for a paper if and only if all of the following
  are satisfied: (a) the paper has at least 3 reviews, (b) no review has
  grade UNDECIDED, and (c) all submitted reviews are consistent
  (either all ACCEPT or all REJECT). Returns 1 on success, otherwise -1
  and leaves the paper's decision unchanged.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate paper != null and decision is either ACCEPT or REJECT.
  2. If paper.reviews.size() < 3, return -1.
  3. For each r in paper.reviews:
       if r.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED, return -1.
  4. If !paper.isAllReviewsConsistent(), return -1.
  5. paper.decision = decision.
  6. Return 1.
Related Features:
  - Paper.decision (Grade)
  - Paper.reviews (List<ReviewAssignment>)
  - Paper.isAllReviewsConsistent()
  - ReviewAssignment.grade (Grade)
Related Classes:
  Paper, ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
  paper : Paper   - the paper whose decision is to be made. Non-null.
  decision : Grade - the final decision; must be ACCEPT or REJECT
             (not UNDECIDED).
Output:
  int - 1 if the decision was successfully applied, -1 otherwise.
Pre-condition:
  - paper is a valid, non-null Paper.
  - decision is ACCEPT or REJECT (not UNDECIDED).
Post-condition:
  - If 1 is returned, paper.decision is set to the provided decision.
  - If -1 is returned, paper.decision remains unchanged.
  - CoChair's own state is unchanged.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title conference Model

class Author {
    papers : List<Paper>
    calculateAcceptanceRate() : double
    countSubmittedPapers() : int
    submitPaper(paper : Paper) : void
}
class CoChair {
    makeFinalDecision(paper : Paper, decision : Grade) : int
}
class Paper {
    decision : Grade
    title : String
    type : PaperType
    reviews : List<ReviewAssignment>
    isAllReviewsConsistent() : boolean
}
class ReviewAssignment {
    feedback : String
    grade : Grade
    paper : Paper
}
class Reviewer {
    assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>
    calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore() : double
    calculateUnsubmittedReviews() : int
}
class User {
    name : String
    roles : List<UserRole>
}
abstract class UserRole {
    id : String
}
enum Grade {
    UNDECIDED
    ACCEPT
    REJECT
}
enum PaperType {
    RESEARCH
    EXPERIENCE
}

Author --|> UserRole
Author --> "*" Paper : papers
CoChair --|> UserRole
Paper --> "*" ReviewAssignment : reviews  'opposite: paper
ReviewAssignment --> Paper : paper  'opposite: reviews
Reviewer --|> UserRole
Reviewer --> "*" ReviewAssignment : assignments
User *-- "*" UserRole : roles
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Author::calculateAcceptanceRate
Summary:
  Calculates the acceptance rate of papers submitted by this author.
  The rate is computed as (number of papers whose decision is ACCEPT) /
  (total number of papers submitted by this author). Returns 0.00 when
  the author has submitted no papers.
Algorithm:
  1. Let total = countSubmittedPapers() (i.e., size of this.papers).
  2. If total == 0, return 0.00.
  3. Let accepted = 0.
  4. For each paper p in this.papers:
       if p.decision == Grade.ACCEPT, increment accepted.
  5. Return (double) accepted / total.
Related Features:
  - papers : List<Paper>  (the author's collection of submitted papers)
Related Classes: Paper, Grade
Input:
  None (operation is parameterless; operates on the receiver author's state).
Output:
  double - acceptance rate in the range [0.00, 1.00], with a precision
           tolerance of 0.001. Returns 0.00 when the author has no papers.
Pre-condition:
  - The receiver Author instance must be properly initialized.
  - The papers list may be empty or contain Paper instances; each Paper
    referenced must have a non-null decision value (one of UNDECIDED,
    ACCEPT, REJECT).
Post-condition:
  - The author's state is not modified.
  - Returned value is in [0.00, 1.00]. If papers is empty, returns 0.00.

Author::submitPaper
Summary:
  Adds the given Paper to the author's list of submitted papers,
  recording a new submission in the system.
Algorithm:
  1. If paper == null, throw IllegalArgumentException (or no-op, depending
     on policy). Otherwise:
  2. Append paper to this.papers.
  3. Optionally establish a back-reference (paper.author = this) so that
     the Paper knows its submitter, ensuring bidirectional consistency.
Related Features:
  - papers : List<Paper>  (the author's collection of submitted papers)
Related Classes: Paper
Input:
  paper : Paper - the paper to be submitted. Must be a non-null Paper
          instance. The paper's title, type, and initial decision
          (typically UNDECIDED or null) are expected to be set by the
          caller before submission.
Output:
  void - no return value.
Pre-condition:
  - paper must not be null.
  - The receiver Author must be properly initialized with a non-null
    papers list.
  - The paper should not already be present in this.papers (to avoid
    duplicate submissions).
Post-condition:
  - The given paper is appended to this.papers.
  - The size of this.papers increases by 1 (assuming no duplicate).
  - The paper is now associated with this author as a submitted paper.

Author::countSubmittedPapers
Summary:
  Returns the total number of papers that have been submitted by this
  author.
Algorithm:
  1. Return this.papers.size().
Related Features:
  - papers : List<Paper>  (the author's collection of submitted papers)
Related Classes: Paper
Input:
  None (parameterless; operates on the receiver author's state).
Output:
  int - non-negative integer representing the number of papers submitted
        by this author. Returns 0 when no paper has been submitted yet.
Pre-condition:
  - The receiver Author instance must be properly initialized with a
    non-null papers list (possibly empty).
Post-condition:
  - The author's state is not modified.
  - The returned value equals the number of Paper instances currently
    associated with this author.

```plantuml
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Reviewer::calculateUnsubmittedReviews
Summary:
  Calculates the number of unsubmitted reviews currently assigned to
  this reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is
  UNDECIDED. Returns 0 if all reviews are submitted or if the reviewer
  has no assignments.
Algorithm:
  1. Let count = 0.
  2. For each ReviewAssignment ra in this.assignments:
       If ra.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED, increment count.
  3. Return count.
Related Features:
  - assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>  (the reviewer's assigned reviews)
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
  None (parameterless; operates on the receiver reviewer's state).
Output:
  int - non-negative integer equal to the number of ReviewAssignment
        entries whose grade is UNDECIDED. Returns 0 when the reviewer
        has no assignments or when all assigned reviews have been
        submitted.
Pre-condition:
  - The receiver Reviewer must be properly initialized with a non-null
    assignments list (possibly empty).
  - Each ReviewAssignment in assignments must have a non-null grade value.
Post-condition:
  - The reviewer's state is not modified.
  - The returned value is in the range [0, assignments.size()].

Reviewer::calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore
Summary:
  Converts this reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical
  average score between 0.0 and 1.0. Only submitted reviews are
  considered (those whose grade is not UNDECIDED). ACCEPT is treated as
  1 and REJECT as 0. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED
  or no assignments), returns 0.00.
Algorithm:
  1. Let sum = 0, count = 0.
  2. For each ReviewAssignment ra in this.assignments:
       a. If ra.grade == Grade.ACCEPT, sum += 1, count += 1.
       b. Else if ra.grade == Grade.REJECT, sum += 0, count += 1.
       c. Else (ra.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED), skip.
  3. If count == 0, return 0.00.
  4. Return (double) sum / count.
Related Features:
  - assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>  (the reviewer's assigned reviews)
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
  None (parameterless; operates on the receiver reviewer's state).
Output:
  double - the average score in the range [0.00, 1.00], within an error
           margin of 0.001. Returns 0.00 when there are no submitted
           reviews.
Pre-condition:
  - The receiver Reviewer must be properly initialized with a non-null
    assignments list (possibly empty).
  - Each ReviewAssignment in assignments must have a non-null grade value
    (one of UNDECIDED, ACCEPT, REJECT).
Post-condition:
  - The reviewer's state is not modified.
  - The returned value lies in [0.00, 1.00]; if no submitted reviews
    exist, the returned value is exactly 0.00.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Operation Specifications
Paper::isAllReviewsConsistent
Summary:
  Determines whether all reviews for this paper are consistent, i.e.,
  either exclusively ACCEPT or exclusively REJECT. If the paper has no
  reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered
  consistent and the method returns false.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.reviews is null or this.reviews.isEmpty(), return false.
  2. Initialize firstGrade = this.reviews.get(0).grade.
  3. If firstGrade == Grade.UNDECIDED, return false.
  4. For each ReviewAssignment ra in this.reviews:
       a. If ra.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED, return false.
       b. If ra.grade != firstGrade, return false.
  5. Return true.
Related Features:
  - reviews : List<ReviewAssignment>  (the paper's collection of reviews)
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
  None (parameterless; operates on the receiver paper's state).
Output:
  boolean - true if there is at least one review and every review has the
            same non-UNDECIDED grade (all ACCEPT or all REJECT);
            false otherwise (including when reviews is empty or any
            review is UNDECIDED).
Pre-condition:
  - The receiver Paper must be properly initialized with a non-null
    reviews list.
  - Each ReviewAssignment in reviews must have a non-null grade value.
Post-condition:
  - The paper's state is not modified.
  - The returned boolean reflects the consistency of the current set of
    reviews at the moment of invocation.

```text
2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.
```



# Operation Specifications
CoChair::makeFinalDecision
Summary:
  Attempts to set the final decision for a given paper. The decision is
  committed (and 1 is returned) only when all three conditions are met:
    (a) the paper has at least 3 reviews,
    (b) no review has grade UNDECIDED,
    (c) all reviews are consistent (either all ACCEPT or all REJECT).
  Otherwise, the paper's decision is left unchanged and -1 is returned.
Algorithm:
  1. If paper == null or decision == null, return -1.
  2. Let reviews = paper.reviews.
  3. If reviews.size() < 3, return -1.
  4. If paper.isAllReviewsConsistent() == false, return -1.
     (isAllReviewsConsistent also returns false if any review is UNDECIDED
      or if there are no reviews, so condition (b) is implicitly enforced.)
  5. Set paper.decision = decision.
  6. Return 1.
Related Features:
  - (CoChair itself has no own attributes; this operation delegates to
    Paper's state.)
Related Classes: Paper, Grade
Input:
  paper    : Paper - the paper for which the final decision is to be made.
             Must be non-null and must have at least 3 associated
             ReviewAssignment entries.
  decision : Grade - the proposed final decision. Must be non-null and is
             expected to be either Grade.ACCEPT or Grade.REJECT.
Output:
  int - 1 if the decision was successfully applied; -1 if any of the
        required conditions failed and the decision was not applied.
Pre-condition:
  - The CoChair must be properly initialized.
  - paper must be non-null and have a non-null reviews list.
  - decision must be non-null.
  - The paper must have at least 3 reviews, all of which must have been
    submitted (no UNDECIDED grades), and all must be consistent (all
    ACCEPT or all REJECT).
Post-condition:
  - On success (return 1): paper.decision == decision.
  - On failure (return -1): paper.decision remains unchanged from its
    previous value.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

```java
   *   1 and REJECT as 0. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED
   *   or no assignments), returns 0.00.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Let sum = 0, count = 0.
   *   2. For each ReviewAssignment ra in this.assignments:
   *        a. If ra.grade == Grade.ACCEPT, sum += 1, count += 1.
   *        b. Else if ra.grade == Grade.REJECT, sum += 0, count += 1.
   *        c. Else (ra.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED), skip.
   *   3. If count == 0, return 0.00.
   *   4. Return (double) sum / count.
   * Related Features:
   *   - assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>  (the reviewer's assigned reviews)
   * Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
   * Input:
   *   None (parameterless; operates on the receiver reviewer's state).
   * Output:
   *   double - the average score in the range [0.00, 1.00], within an error
   *            margin of 0.001. Returns 0.00 when there are no submitted
   *            reviews.
   * Pre-condition:
   *   - The receiver Reviewer must be properly initialized with a non-null
   *     assignments list (possibly empty).
   *   - Each ReviewAssignment in assignments must have a non-null grade value
   *     (one of UNDECIDED, ACCEPT, REJECT).
   * Post-condition:
   *   - The reviewer's state is not modified.
```

```java
   *        If ra.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED, increment count.
   *   3. Return count.
```

    for (ReviewAssignment ra : getAssignments())

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   *   Converts this reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical
   *   average score between 0.0 and 1.0. Only submitted reviews are
   *   considered (those whose grade is not UNDECIDED). ACCEPT is treated as
   *   1 and REJECT as 0. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED
   *   or no assignments), returns 0.00.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Let sum = 0, count = 0.
   *   2. For each ReviewAssignment ra in this.assignments:
   *        a. If ra.grade == Grade.ACCEPT, sum += 1, count += 1.
   *        b. Else if ra.grade == Grade.REJECT, sum += 0, count += 1.
   *        c. Else (ra.grade == Grade.UNDECIDED), skip.
   *   3. If count == 0, return 0.00.
   *   4. Return (double) sum / count.
   * Related Features:
   *   - assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>  (the reviewer's assigned reviews)
   * Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
   * Input:
   *   None (parameterless; operates on the receiver reviewer's state).
   * Output:
   *   double - the average score in the range [0.00, 1.00], within an error
   *            margin of 0.001. Returns 0.00 when there are no submitted
   *            reviews.
   * Pre-condition:
   *   - The receiver Reviewer must be properly initialized with a non-null
   *     assignments list (possibly empty).
   *   - Each ReviewAssignment in assignments must have a non-null grade value
   *     (one of UNDECIDED, ACCEPT, REJECT).
   * Post-condition:
   *   - The reviewer's state is not modified.
   *   - The returned value lies in [0.00, 1.00]; if no submitted reviews
   *     exist, the returned value is exactly 0.00.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore()
  {
    int sum = 0;
    int count = 0;
    for (ReviewAssignment ra : getAssignments())
    {
      if (ra.getGrade() == Grade.ACCEPT)
      {
        sum += 1;
        count += 1;
      }
      else if (ra.getGrade() == Grade.REJECT)
      {
        sum += 0;
        count += 1;
      }
      // Else (UNDECIDED), skip
    }
    if (count == 0)
    {
      return 0.00;
    }
    return (double) sum / count;
  }

```java
   *   committed (and 1 is returned) only when all three conditions are met:
   *     (a) the paper has at least 3 reviews,
   *     (b) no review has grade UNDECIDED,
   *     (c) all reviews are consistent (either all ACCEPT or all REJECT).
   *   Otherwise, the paper's decision is left unchanged and -1 is returned.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If paper == null or decision == null, return -1.
   *   2. Let reviews = paper.reviews.
   *   3. If reviews.size() < 3, return -1.
   *   4. If paper.isAllReviewsConsistent() == false, return -1.
   *      (isAllReviewsConsistent also returns false if any review is UNDECIDED
   *       or if there are no reviews, so condition (b) is implicitly enforced.)
   *   5. Set paper.decision = decision.
   *   6. Return 1.
   * Related Features:
   *   - (CoChair itself has no own attributes; this operation delegates to
   *     Paper's state.)
   * Related Classes: Paper, Grade
   * Input:
   *   paper    : Paper - the paper for which the final decision is to be made.
   *              Must be non-null.
   *   decision : Grade - the proposed final decision. Must be non-null and is
   *              expected to be either Grade.ACCEPT or Grade.REJECT.
   * Output:
   *   int - 1 if the decision was successfully applied; -1 if any of the
   *         required conditions failed and the decision was not applied.
   * Pre-condition:
   *   - The CoChair must be properly initialized.
   *   - paper must be non-null and have a non-null reviews list.
   *   - decision must be non-null.
   * Post-condition:
   *   - On success (return 1): paper.decision == decision.
   *   - On failure (return -1): paper.decision remains unchanged from its
   *     previous value.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
```

   *     Paper's state.)
   * Related Classes: Paper, Grade
   * Input:
   *   paper    : Paper - the paper for which the final decision is to be made.
   *              Must be non-null.
   *   decision : Grade - the proposed final decision. Must be non-null and is
   *              expected to be either Grade.ACCEPT or Grade.REJECT.
   * Output:
   *   int - 1 if the decision was successfully applied; -1 if any of the
   *         required conditions failed and the decision was not applied.
   * Pre-condition:
   *   - The CoChair must be properly initialized.
   *   - paper must be non-null and have a non-null reviews list.
   *   - decision must be non-null.
   * Post-condition:
   *   - On success (return 1): paper.decision == decision.
   *   - On failure (return -1): paper.decision remains unchanged from its
   *     previous value.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public int makeFinalDecision(final Paper paper, final Grade decision)
  {
    if (paper == null || decision == null)
    {
      return -1;
    }
    if (paper.getReviews().size() < 3)
    {
      return -1;
    }
    if (!paper.isAllReviewsConsistent())
    {
      return -1;
    }
    paper.setDecision(decision);
    return 1;
  }

} // CoChair

  {
    EList<ReviewAssignment> paperReviews = getReviews();
    if (paperReviews == null || paperReviews.isEmpty())
    {
      return false;
    }
    Grade firstGrade = paperReviews.get(0).getGrade();
    if (firstGrade == Grade.UNDECIDED)
    {
      return false;
    }
    for (ReviewAssignment ra : paperReviews)
    {
      if (ra.getGrade() == Grade.UNDECIDED)
      {
        return false;
      }
      if (ra.getGrade() != firstGrade)
      {
        return false;
      }
    }
    return true;
  }
